# maps.geo serves emulator-authored basemap assets instead of the published style, sprite and glyph release (7 causes)

> _Note: this issue was identified against the code base at commit `789c7a890d26c428a053c99e86a00990d57bc346`. If you are an issue fixer working on this, the code base you are currently working in can have drifted, so you might need to trace the issue back to the current code base._

## Operation
GetGlyphs

## Description
Every one of the 12 replayed maps.geo wire steps diverges (steps_passed 0 / steps_total 12, score 0.0%). The status line is always right (200 -> 200) and the modelled envelope is right, but the @httpPayload BLOB each of the three asset readers serves is computed by the emulator rather than being the published basemap asset for the requested labels. This bundle carries 7 net-new causes. Two EARLIER issues on this same fault family are RESOLVED and therefore cover none of these: 20261029T182815Z-maps-geo-serves-synthetic-glyph-and-sprite-assets.md and 20261030T062941Z-maps-geo-getstyledescriptor-serves-a-synthesized-style-document.md. The emulator's values have moved since those were filed (GetGlyphs now answers 139609 bytes where that issue recorded 121491), so the asset pipeline was touched but still does not serve the recorded release.

Cause 1 of 7 - GetGlyphs serves a glyph PBF that is not the glyph set for the requested font stack and unicode range.
AWS: Smithy GetGlyphs -> GET /v2/glyphs/{FontStack}/{FontUnicodeRange}; FontStack @length max 1000, FontUnicodeRange @length max 50 @pattern ^[0-9]+-[0-9]+\.pbf$, both @required @httpLabel; output GetGlyphsResponse.Blob is the @httpPayload with Content-Type / Cache-Control / ETag @httpHeaders; the model declares NO errors. The model says nothing about the bytes, so for the body: recorded behavior only - the recording shows the glyph protobuf for that (FontStack, FontUnicodeRange) pair, a body whose length is a function of those two labels.
Emulator: 200 with a body of a different length and digest for the same two labels - __wire_length__ expected 74786, got 139609; __wire_sha256__ expected 73b6ca0b7819f2dc763c75c5798d6fcb8527e74105de50744dbe32138c26ac18, got 93b5b015200340cdd49f52bc5c9ef34bb8d0054e168311726b3385b3d20ee6b6. (Those numbers are this recording's values, not the requirement; the requirement is that the body be the glyph set for the requested stack and range.)
Why: a MapLibre/GL client reads per-glyph advance and SDF bitmap data out of this PBF. A body that is not the glyph set for the requested range makes every label lay out wrongly or fail to decode, and the ETag/Cache-Control pair then caches that wrong body under a key the real service uses for the correct one.
Fix: the GetGlyphs handler and the glyph asset source it reads - services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_glyphs.rs and the `crate::state::assets` glyph routines in services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.rs; the blob must be looked up from the published glyph release keyed by FontStack and FontUnicodeRange rather than generated.
Traces: geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges#task#1 (cluster also covers that fixture's task#2 and task#3 and geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#4).

Cause 2 of 7 - GetSprites serves a sprite PNG sheet that is not the sheet published for the requested Style/ColorScheme/Variant.
AWS: Smithy GetSprites -> GET /v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/{FileName}; FileName @required @pattern ^sprites(@2x)?\.(png|json)$, Style (MapStyle: Hybrid|Monochrome|Satellite|Standard), ColorScheme (Dark|Light) and Variant (Default) all @required enum @httpLabels; output Blob is the @httpPayload, no modelled errors. For the pixels: recorded behavior only - the recording shows the published sprite sheet for those labels.
Emulator: 200 with a far smaller, different image - __wire_length__ expected 121655, got 20535; __wire_sha256__ expected f0d727b230e676e2a204c4f7ec0ab50ff2c73ead3945df40d2532ae0d4746b54, got 64431505ef0230d0144779532d8831c5cd7a6b35bda373fafca5e26afd23819c. src/ops/get_sprites.rs:207-209 shows why: the body is `assets::render_sprite_sheet(&assets::layout_sprites(&sheet, pixel_ratio))`, i.e. an atlas DRAWN from a computed layout, not a stored sheet object.
Why: a renderer slices each icon out of this image using the rectangles in the companion sprites.json. An image that is not the published sheet means every POI, marker and route-shield icon is cut from the wrong pixels - blank or garbage symbols across the whole map.
Fix: the GetSprites handler's Sheet branch and the sprite asset source - src/ops/get_sprites.rs plus `assets::render_sprite_sheet` / the `sprites` catalog records in src/state.rs; serve the stored sheet object for the requested Style/ColorScheme/Variant/FileName instead of rendering one.
Traces: geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants#task#1 (cluster also covers that fixture's task#2 and geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#2).

Cause 3 of 7 - GetSprites sprites.json publishes a computed uniform-grid geometry instead of the published sheet's per-icon rectangles (Monochrome/Dark request).
AWS: recorded behavior only. sprites.json is a map of icon name -> {x, y, width, height, pixelRatio}; each icon's size and offset are fixed by the published sheet, sizes VARY by icon class, and x advances along a row rather than wrapping on a fixed pitch.
Emulator: emits one generated grid - icons AWS publishes at 21x21 come out 24x32 (marker, marker_arts_entertainment, marker_food_drink, marker_recreation, marker_stores_services, marker_transit), icons AWS publishes at a class-specific size are flattened to 21x21 (oneway 50x29 -> 21x21, other 32x32 -> 21x21), and the x offsets drift monotonically with the wrong pitch (metro.x 936 -> 954, money.x 957 -> 975, monorail.x 978 -> 996, museum.x 999 -> 1017 ... then reverse once a wrongly sized icon is passed: other.x 1112 -> 1101, outdoor.x 1144 -> 1122, park.x 1165 -> 1143, place-of-worship.x 1228 -> 1206). Hundreds of entries diverge, including the whole shield-* family's width/height/x/y. src/ops/get_sprites.rs:207,210 shows the index is `assets::sprite_index_json(&assets::layout_sprites(...))` - derived from the same computed layout as cause 2, contradicting that file's own module doc, which claims the index is "that record's own stored geometry ... rather than an atlas computed per request".
Why: this JSON is the index into the PNG. A wrong width/height crops or over-reads an icon and a wrong x/y picks its neighbour, so a client pairing the emulator's index with any real sheet draws the wrong symbol at the wrong size for essentially every icon.
Fix: `assets::layout_sprites` / `assets::sprite_index_json` in src/state.rs, consumed by src/ops/get_sprites.rs - the geometry must be read from the sheet record's stored rectangles, not recomputed as a grid.
Traces: geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants#task#3.

Cause 4 of 7 - the same computed sprites.json geometry on a different Style/ColorScheme request, confirming the layout does not vary with the labels.
AWS: recorded behavior only, as in cause 3 - per-icon geometry fixed by the published sheet for the REQUESTED Style/ColorScheme/Variant, so two different sheets have different geometry.
Emulator: the identical per-icon divergences as cause 3 (marker family 21x21 -> 24x32, oneway 50x29 -> 21x21, other 32x32 -> 21x21, the same monotonic x drift, the same shield-* family) on a different request, which shows the grid is computed once from icon-name ordering and is independent of the sheet requested.
Why: as cause 3, plus a client cannot tell one style/colour-scheme variant from another, because their indexes are byte-identical where AWS publishes different layouts.
Fix: same component as cause 3; the lookup must be keyed by Style/ColorScheme/Variant/FileName and return that record's stored geometry.
Traces: geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#3.

Cause 5 of 7 - GetStyleDescriptor's layers are emulator-authored: filter expressions, source-layer bindings, paint colours and zoom-stop ramps do not match the published style (Standard/Dark request).
AWS: Smithy GetStyleDescriptor -> GET /v2/styles/{Style}/descriptor with ColorScheme/PoliticalView/Terrain/ContourDensity/Traffic/TravelModes/Buildings/PoiDensity/PoiCategories as @httpQuery knobs; output Blob is the @httpPayload, no modelled errors. For the document: recorded behavior only - the recording is the published MapLibre style document, in which each layer's `filter`, `source-layer`, `layout` and `paint` come from the style release.
Emulator: serves a different document under the same 200. Per-layer examples from the replay: layers[id="rail_tunnel"].filter[1][1][1] 'kind' -> 'role'; filter[1][2][0] 'transit' -> 'way'; filter[1][2][1] 'rail' -> MISSING; filter[2][0] 'has' -> '=='; filter[2][1] 'is_tunnel' -> ['geometry-type']; filter[2][2] extra 'LineString'; filter[3] ['!',['has','service']], filter[4] the ['case',...'kind_detail'...] clause and filter[5] ['==',['geometry-type'],'LineString'] all MISSING; paint.line-color 'hsl(196, 10%, 18%)' -> 'hsl(213, 7%, 25%)'; paint.line-width[3] 15 -> 12, [6] 1.5 -> 3.0, and stops [7]=20 / [8]=2 MISSING. The same shape of divergence repeats across hundreds of layers (admin_country*, admin_label_*, admin_region*, airport_*, bridge_* ...) and includes `source-layer` names and `minzoom`/`maxzoom`.
Why: the style document IS the rendering program. Wrong filters put features in the wrong layers or drop them, a wrong source-layer binds a layer to a vector-tile layer that does not exist, and truncated line-width interpolation stops make line weights snap instead of ramping - so a client renders a map that does not match AWS at any zoom, and a client that asserts on style content sees a document the service never published.
Fix: the GetStyleDescriptor handler's document source - `assets::style_descriptor` and the `styles` catalog records in src/state.rs, called from src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs:285-290; the layer array must be the published style release for the requested Style/ColorScheme and knobs, not synthesized layers.
Traces: geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options#task#1.

Cause 6 of 7 - the same authored-layer divergence on a second Style/ColorScheme (Monochrome/Light).
AWS: recorded behavior only, as in cause 5 - the published style document for the requested Style and ColorScheme.
Emulator: the identical structural divergences on the Monochrome/Light request (rail_tunnel / rail_tie_tunnel filters rewritten the same way, the same missing filter[3..5] clauses, the same truncated line-width stops) with only the colour literals re-tinted (paint.line-color 'hsl(228, 0%, 91%)' -> 'hsl(228, 0%, 100%)'). That shows one authored layer template is re-coloured per scheme rather than each scheme's published document being served.
Why: as cause 5, and a client cannot rely on any per-style difference beyond colour, because every other property is shared from the one template.
Fix: same component as cause 5; keyed lookup of the published document per (Style, ColorScheme) rather than a re-tinted template.
Traces: geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options#task#2.

Cause 7 of 7 - GetStyleDescriptor pastes the CALLER'S request authority into the document's `sprite` template, so the published asset reference is an IP-literal loopback URL.
AWS: recorded behavior only - the style document's `sprite` member is asserted by shape (expected '<shaped-not-asserted>'), i.e. the recorded value has the form of a maps.geo asset reference and not an absolute URL rooted at whatever Host the caller sent.
Emulator: $.sprite is 'https://127.0.0.1:<port>/v2/styles/<Style>/<ColorScheme>/Default/sprites/sprites' - an absolute URL whose authority is built from the request's URI host or Host header by `request_endpoint` (src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs:317-356) and handed to `assets::style_descriptor` as the template root. The specific host and port differ per run and are NOT the finding; the finding is the structural choice to root the reference at a client-supplied authority (here an IP literal with a port), which does not have the shape of the published reference. The failure reproduces on two different runs/ports, so it is not a one-recording artifact.
Why: a client that stores or forwards the style document (a cached style, a style served through a proxy, a style handed to another renderer) ends up with a sprite reference pointing at an address that means nothing outside the one process that answered, where AWS's reference stays resolvable. It also makes the document's content depend on the request's Host header, so the same style is not byte-stable.
Fix: src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs `request_endpoint` and the `assets::style_descriptor` template substitution in src/state.rs - emit the asset reference in the form the published document uses rather than rooting it at the caller's authority.
Traces: geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#1 (this cluster's sole divergence is $.sprite; the same $.sprite divergence also rides along in causes 5 and 6).

## Evidence
Replay of trace set simparity-wire-aws-v1 against the emulator built from this checkout: emulator_up true, bootstrap_signal false, fixtures_replayed 4, steps_total 12, steps_passed 0, steps_mismatched 12, divergence_count 7, score_pct 0.0. All 7 clusters are service=maps.geo, verdict=mismatch, expected_status 200 and actual_status 200, with stub_path and owned_by both empty.

1) divergence_key service=maps.geo|GetGlyphs|status=200->200|keys=$.__wire_length__,$.__wire_sha256__ - fixture geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges step task#1 (affected_count 4). Expected __wire_length__ 74786 / sha256 73b6ca0b7819f2dc763c75c5798d6fcb8527e74105de50744dbe32138c26ac18; got 139609 / 93b5b015200340cdd49f52bc5c9ef34bb8d0054e168311726b3385b3d20ee6b6.

2) divergence_key service=maps.geo|GetSprites|status=200->200|keys=$.__wire_length__,$.__wire_sha256__ - fixture geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants step task#1 (affected_count 3). Expected 121655 / f0d727b230e676e2a204c4f7ec0ab50ff2c73ead3945df40d2532ae0d4746b54; got 20535 / 64431505ef0230d0144779532d8831c5cd7a6b35bda373fafca5e26afd23819c.

3) divergence_key service=maps.geo|GetSprites|status=200->200|keys=$.marker.height,$.marker.width,... - fixture geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants step task#3. Sample: marker.width 21->24, marker.height 21->32, marker_food_drink.x 852->858, oneway 50x29->21x21, other 32x32->21x21, other.x 1112->1101, park.x 1165->1143, place-of-worship.x 1228->1206, plus the whole shield-* family's width/height/x/y.

4) divergence_key service=maps.geo|GetSprites|status=200->200|keys=$.marker.height,... - fixture geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow step task#3. The same per-icon width/height/x divergences as (3) on a different Style/ColorScheme request.

5) divergence_key service=maps.geo|GetStyleDescriptor|status=200->200|keys=$.layers[id="admin_country"]... - fixture geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options step task#1. Sample: layers[id="rail_tunnel"].filter[1][1][1] 'kind'->'role'; filter[1][2][0] 'transit'->'way'; filter[1][2][1] 'rail'->missing; filter[2][0] 'has'->'=='; filter[2][1] 'is_tunnel'->['geometry-type']; filter[3] ['!',['has','service']]->missing; filter[5] ['==',['geometry-type'],'LineString']->missing; paint.line-color 'hsl(196, 10%, 18%)'->'hsl(213, 7%, 25%)'; paint.line-width[3] 15->12, [6] 1.5->3.0, [7] 20->missing, [8] 2->missing. The divergence_key lists hundreds more layers (admin_country*, admin_label_*, admin_region*, airport_*, bridge_motorway-trunk* ...) across filter, layout, paint, minzoom/maxzoom and source-layer.

6) divergence_key service=maps.geo|GetStyleDescriptor|status=200->200|keys=$.layers[...] - fixture geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options step task#2. Same structural divergences, colour literal differs: paint.line-color 'hsl(228, 0%, 91%)'->'hsl(228, 0%, 100%)'.

7) divergence_key service=maps.geo|GetStyleDescriptor|status=200->200|keys=$.sprite - fixture geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow step task#1. $.sprite: expected '<shaped-not-asserted>', got 'https://127.0.0.1:35699/v2/styles/Standard/Light/Default/sprites/sprites'. The same member also diverged in (5) as 'https://127.0.0.1:41125/v2/styles/Standard/Dark/Default/sprites/sprites' and in (6) as 'https://127.0.0.1:41125/v2/styles/Monochrome/Light/Default/sprites/sprites' - two different runs/ports, so the shape, not the port, is the divergence.

Model evidence (smithy_query query="operation" on services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/smithy-model/com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice.json): GetGlyphs GET /v2/glyphs/{FontStack}/{FontUnicodeRange}, FontUnicodeRange @pattern ^[0-9]+-[0-9]+\.pbf$, output Blob @httpPayload, errors []; GetSprites GET /v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/{FileName}, FileName @pattern ^sprites(@2x)?\.(png|json)$, output Blob @httpPayload, errors []; GetStyleDescriptor GET /v2/styles/{Style}/descriptor, output Blob @httpPayload, errors []. The model constrains the labels and the envelope only - it says nothing about asset bytes, so the bodies above are graded as recorded behavior.

Code evidence: src/ops/get_sprites.rs:207-211 builds both the PNG and the JSON index from `assets::layout_sprites(&sheet, file.pixel_ratio)` (computed atlas), while that file's own module doc (lines 8-11) states the index is the record's stored geometry "rather than an atlas computed per request". src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs:285-290 passes `request_endpoint(ctx)` (lines 317-356, host taken from the request URI or Host header) into `assets::style_descriptor` as the document's URL root.

## Steps to Reproduce
1. git clone the repo and build/boot the emulator from this checkout (the data listener and /__admin/health come up; bootstrap_signal is false).
2. Replay trace set simparity-wire-aws-v1 (replay_wire_corpus). All 12 maps.geo steps mismatch; 7 divergence clusters are reported.
3. Cause 1: replay geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges step task#1 - GET /v2/glyphs/{FontStack}/{FontUnicodeRange} with the fixture's labels; compare the response body length and sha256 with the recorded ones (74786 / 73b6ca0b...ac18).
4. Cause 2: replay geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants step task#1 - GET /v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/sprites.png; compare body length and sha256 with the recorded ones (121655 / f0d727b2...6b54).
5. Causes 3 and 4: replay geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants step task#3 and geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow step task#3 - GET .../sprites/sprites.json; compare each icon's x/y/width/height against the recorded index, and compare the two responses with each other to see that the geometry does not vary with Style/ColorScheme.
6. Causes 5 and 6: replay geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options steps task#1 and task#2 - GET /v2/styles/{Style}/descriptor?color-scheme=...&<knobs>; diff the layers array against the recorded document (start at layers[id="rail_tunnel"].filter and .paint.line-width).
7. Cause 7: replay geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow step task#1 and read $.sprite in the response body; it is an absolute URL rooted at the Host used to call the emulator. Repeat the call with a different Host header to see the member's value change with the request.
